Let's think of computational approaches.

1. Continuous density optimization:

Instead of manual, implement numerical continuous optimization. Let's design an algorithm that will definitely lower <1.5.

Potential approach 1: Smooth optimization on moderate length using evolutionary/LS.

We can write propose_candidate:
- If time available, use an iterative convex-like solver on p of length N (e.g., 512 or 1024) to minimize smooth max surrogate.
- Use multiple restarts: initial profiles: uniform + random Fourier; optimized two-harmonic; maybe "asymmetric tent".
- Then take best p length N, upsample/resize to length n_target (maybe 2048 or 6733) by linear interpolation or repeated sampling, normalize, final local search with evaluate_sequence or internal exact conv for N_small? Could evaluate final with exact for selected lengths.
- Ensure return best found finite.

Need decide n_target. The evaluator includes n factor, but objective invariant to scaling shape? If we interpolate shape to different n, approximate continuous same; finite edge effects differ. We can test at runtime by evaluating several lengths (e.g. 1024,2048,4096,6733,8192) using exact evaluate_sequence (direct convolve cost O(n^2)). For 8192, convolution 67M operations, maybe 0.5s each; few okay. Use internal FFT for many. We can return length with best exact score. The task says larger sequences often have better attack surface; but too large too slow. We can choose 4096 or 6733. For 6733 direct eval maybe ~45M, acceptable maybe 0.1-0.3s in C? np.convolve direct for float64 uses Python loop? Actually np.convolve for 1D uses C direct? It likely uses direct O(n*m) C loop; 45M multiplications maybe 0.05-0.2s. So we can evaluate many final candidates up to 10k. Use 6733 maybe given length. But if optimized profile for N=2048, upsample to 6733; score similar. Could simply return the optimized profile at length L=2048; score ~1.5? Need exact. Larger n edge effects less? For continuous approximation, E~2M. If M~0.75, any length works. Smaller n may have discrete quantization cannot make qmax lower than 1/n? Minimum max q ≥ average=1/(2n), so E≥2n/(2n)=1. But finite constraints? Larger n discreteness fine. Return maybe 2048 for lower final eval cost. But the harnes expects length? any. Use maybe 4096 to reduce discrete effects and still eval okay. However if local search on final length with direct eval too slow; internal FFT can handle 4096.

Let's implement a generic discrete optimizer.

Detailed optimizer design:

Function _fft_conv(A,B=None): if B None square. Use next power of two >=2n-1? For linear convolution length 2n-1. Use scipy.fft? import numpy.fft or scipy.fft (faster). Use numpy allowed. rfft length N. For square: fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N); q = np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, N)[:2*n-1]. Use double. Tiny negative due to FFT; clip to 0 for objective? Since exact conv nonnegative; FFT errors can be small negative. Use np.maximum(q,0.0). For gradient, use q with tiny negative? Keep q as computed maybe max(0). Good.

Objective score internal = 2*n*max(q)/S^2. But if we internally normalize p with S=1, score=2*n*max(q). Use q from p sum1.

Smoothing: Use log-sum-exp. For p sum1 and q length 2n-1. Let m = max(q). r = exp(β(q - m))? For gradient of L = (1/β) log ∑ exp(β q). Let u=β q, u_max. r=exp(u-u_max), sum_R. Derivative L w.r.t q = r/sum_R (normalized softmax). Then G=A_i+ p_i (if r sum=1). Actually if derivative of L is softmax normalized, ∑ r=1. Good.
Update logp: logp_i <- logp_i - lr * (G_i - ∑ p_i G_i). But G depends on r. Learning rate: need scale. Since p_i ~1/n, G ~∑ r (p+p) ~2/n maybe. Weighted mean ~2/n. lr should be O(n). Could normalize by mean p? Use relative update: logp_i -= lr * (G_i - Gbar) / (2/n?) = lr * (G_i - Gbar)* n/2. Choose lr in [0.05, 0.5]. Could use Adam on logp: gradθ_i=G_i - Gbar; update Adam. But logsumexp gradient magnitude maybe small; Adam robust. Implement simple RMSProp:
   m_v = β1 m_v+(1-β1) grad^2; v = sqrt(m_v+eps); step = lr * grad/v? But grad mostly constant scale; maybe use lr ~0.1.
Need choose β adaptation: Start β=50? Since q scale 1/n. For n=1024, qmax~0.001, β=1000 -> βq~1. Need β maybe 10000. We can dynamically set β = target_ratio / qmax, e.g. 50 / qmax. But as qmax ~1/n, β ~50n. For n=1024 ~51200. Exponents in [-50,0], fine. Use high β later.
However logsumexp with β huge tracks max; gradient only from peak bins; may get stuck. Use β schedule: start 20/qmax? then increase.

But direct gradient on logp may be slow. We can also do "peak shaving" coordinate moves:
Given p and q, m=max. For indices in p that are in large q, decrease them proportional to gradient at peak; increase low-impact indices. This is a Frank-Wolfe / projected gradient.

Projected gradient in p space: p∈simplex. Gradient of smooth L wrt p is G (length n). Projected step: p_new = projection onto simplex of p - η G. Projection onto simplex for nonnegative sum1 can be done in O(n log n) or O(n) sort. This is simpler and controls step. G_i values O(1/n), q differences O(1/n), L derivatives O(1/n); η O(n) maybe. Use scaled G = (G-Gbar) * n? Because simplex gradient gradient of L wrt p has components O(1/n); optimal step for L1? We can use η = step_size * (1 / max(|G|))? Or use standard FISTA? Let's think.
We minimize F(p)=logsumexp(β q)/β on simplex. Gradient ∂F/∂p = G_i where r normalized; G_i≈ (1/n)*something. The Lipschitz constant maybe O(β?) Could set η = 1/(λ) where λ estimated from G changes. Easier: use normalized gradient H_i = (G_i - Gbar)/max(abs(G_i-Gbar)+eps). p_new = proj_simplex(p - η P, simplexescale). But mixing into p with η between 0 and 1: H is shape. Since p entries ~1/n, if we subtract ηH (H O(1)), it will break nonneg for small η? H can be ~1, p ~1/n, so η must <1/n to keep nonnegative. But projected projection clips at zero, it's okay but large change relative to zero. Better scale H to p scale: H_i = (G_i-Gbar)/max|diff| * (1/n). Then step p - α H, α in [0,1]. This adjusts p by O(1/n). Good.
Use FISTA maybe: momentum. Function must be convex? Not globally, but local. We can do simple heavy-ball.
Algorithm per iteration:
   q = conv(p,p)
   m = q.max(); if not finite break
   if βq? x=β*(q-m); r=exp(x); r/=r.sum()
   G = cross_corr(r,p) + p * r.sum() (r.sum=1)
   Gbar = dot(p,G)
   grad = G - Gbar

  best_p=p, best_max=inf, best_F=inf
  lam=0.2
  momentum=0?

     q=fast_conv_square(p); qmax=q.max(); smooth = ... (if needed)
     if qmax < best_max: update best

```
def _evaluate_internal(a):
    n=len(a); s=np.sum(a)
    if s <=0: return inf
    p=a/s
    q=_fft_conv_square(p)
    return 2.0*n*np.max(q)
```

```
Pseudo:
```
def _optimize_mirror(p0, beta, lam, max_time, rng, verbose_tag):
    p = p0.copy(); p = p/p.sum(); theta = np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300))
    n=p.size
    best_p=p.copy(); best_score=_internal_score(p); stale=0
    current_score=best_score
    base_lambda=lam
    beta_cur=beta
    eps=1e-12
    while time < deadline:
       q = _fft_conv_square(p); qmax=q.max(); score=2*n*qmax
       if score < best_score - 1e-9: best... stale=0
       else stale +=1
       if stale > 20:
           # restart with best plus noise or reduce lambda
           lam = max(0.02, lam*0.5)
           if rng.random()<0.3: theta = np.log(best_p + 1e-9) + rng.normal(0,0.2)
           else: theta = np.log(p + 1e-9)
        # compute gradient
       m=qmax
       # beta maybe increase as score close? beta = 50 / max(qmax,1e-12) maybe qmax~1/n. For n=128, qmax~0.002, beta=25000; exponents up to 50.
       beta_cur = 40.0 / max(qmax, 1e-12)
       x = beta_cur * (q - m)
       x -= x.max()
       r=np.exp(x); r/=r.sum()
       A=_corr_r_p(r,p); G=A+p; Gbar=np.dot(p,G); grad=G-Gbar
       scale=np.max(np.abs(grad))
       if scale < 1e-14: break
       step = lam * grad / scale
       # optional Gaussian noise scaled by lam
       if rng.random()<0.2: step += rng.normal(0, lam*0.05)
       theta -= step
       # center theta
       p = _softmax_from_theta(theta)
       # if any nonfinite, reset
    return best_p,best_score
```

```
def _proj_simplex(v):
    v=v.copy(); u=np.sort(v[::-1]); cssv=np.cumsum(u); rho=np.nonzero(u*(np.arange(1,len(u)+1)) - cssv >0)[0][-1]; theta=cssv[rho]/(rho+1); return np.maximum(v-theta,0)
```
Then normalize. This enforces nonnegative sum exactly. The step direction -H. It may be more reliable. H = (G-Gbar)/scale * np.mean(p) (scale of G). Step size alpha. def _optimize_pdgd(p0, tau, beta, rng, ...):
```
    p = p0/p.sum(); best...
    y = p.copy(); momentum=0.9

       q,f = conv,y? For objective use p.

```
Function `_improve_profile(p0, max_iters, deadline, rng, c=20.0, step0=0.5, tag='')`:
- p = p0 normalized
- best_p, best_score, best_q? We can store best_q for gradient? Recompute.
- current = p.copy(), q = conv(current), qmax, score.
- alpha = step0; stale=0
- for it in range(max_iters) while time<deadline:
   q = fft_conv_square(current); qmax = np.max(q); score = 2*n*qmax
   if score < best_score - 1e-10: update best, stale=0
   else: stale +=1
   # If long no improvement, try exploration:
   if stale > 50:
       # restart from best with random smooth perturbation
       current = best_p * np.exp(rng.normal(0, 0.1, n)); current = np.clip(current,0,None)/sum
       alpha = max(0.1, alpha*0.5)
       stale=0
   # compute r, gradient
   # beta: use adaptive c / qmax, but c maybe 20. qmax ~ score/(2n). For n=1024, score1.5 => qmax~0.000732, beta~27300.
   beta = c / max(qmax, 1e-12)
   x=beta*(q-qmax); x-=x.max(); r=np.exp(x); rsum=r.sum(); r/=rsum
   A=_corr_r_p(r,p? current). Use current.
   G=A+current
   Gbar=np.dot(current,G)
   grad=G-Gbar
   scale=np.max(np.abs(grad))
   if scale < 1e-15: break
   direction = grad/scale * (1.0/n)
   # Try a few step sizes to choose best candidate without too much cost? We can evaluate candidates with quick internal score (conv). Maybe 2 steps.
   cand1 = _proj_simplex(current - alpha*direction)
   # maybe cand2 = _proj_simplex(current - 0.5*alpha*direction)
   # Evaluate a candidate that likely best: choose based on surrogate? Could compute internal score for cand1 and if worse maybe smaller.
   # To save, always np.proj_simplex; no score for candidate? Could track and stagnate. Better to evaluate candidate score (another conv) to decide alpha and best. That doubles conv cost.    current = cand1
   # update alpha: if cand_score <= score: alpha = min(1.0, alpha*1.05) else alpha*=0.8
```

Upsampling function:
```
def _resample_profile(p_from, n_to):
    n_from=len(p_from)
    if n_from == n_to: return p_from.copy()
    if n_to < n_from:
        # linear interpolate from density f=p*n_from to p_to density.
        x_old=np.linspace(0,1,n_from); f_old=p_from*n_from
        # For downsample, choose x_new linspace; but interpolation at endpoints.
        x_new=np.linspace(0,1,n_to)
        f_new=np.interp(x_new, x_old, f_old)
    else:
        x_old=...; x_new=...
        f_new=np.interp(...)
    f_new=np.maximum(f_new,0)
    s=f_new.sum()
    if s==0: return np.ones(n_to)/n_to
    return f_new/s
```
For upsample, setting f_old=p_from*n_from. If p_from sum1.

Potential bug: `_proj_simplex` with v all nonnegative? Implementation:
```
def _proj_simplex(v):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.isfinite(v).all(): return np.ones(len(v))/len(v)
    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv = np.cumsum(u)
    rho = np.arange(1, len(v)+1, dtype=float)
    cond = u * rho - cssv > 0
    if not np.any(cond):
        return np.clip(v,0,None)  # maybe normalize
    k = int(np.where(cond)[0][-1])
    theta = cssv[k]/(k+1)
    return np.maximum(v-theta,0.0)
```
Then normalize by sum (should 1 but due to theta). If sum zero uniform.

But projected gradient direction normalized by max diff and scaled by 1/n. If current already simplex, subtract direction maybe some negative but projection ok.

When using gradient G from r/p, if p has zeros, lazy? `corr_r_p` with p works. If Gbar weighted by p (zeros no issue).

```
def _internal_score_direct(a):
   a=np.asarray(a); n=len(a); s=a.sum(); p=a/s; q=np.convolve(p,p); return 2*n*np.max(q)
```
For length 128, fine.

Step grid generation:
```
def _step_search(L, max_time):
   best_p=None; best=inf
   # use direct score for speed
   cs = np.arange(0.05, 0.96, 0.02)
   As = np.arange(0.2, 3.2, 0.05)
   count=0
   for c in cs:
     for A in As:
       B=(1-A*c)/(1-c)
       if B <= 1e-6: continue
       x=np.arange(L)/L  # maybe (i+0.5)/L
       f=np.where(x < c, A, B)
       p=f/f.sum()
       sc=_internal_score_direct(p)
       update
   # refine around best c,A with finer grid if time
```
But c,A two dims; after find best, refine: c range ±0.04 step 0.002, A ±0.2 step 0.005. ~40*80=3200. Good.

Implement combined:
```
def _next_pow2(m): n=1; while n<m: n*=2; return n

def _conv_square_and_corr(p, r=None, N=None):
   n=len(p); m=2*n-1; conv_len=3*n-2
   if N is None: N=_next_pow2(max(m,conv_len))
   fp=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
   q=np.fft.irfft(fp*fp, N)[:m]
   q=np.maximum(q,0)
   if r is None:
       return q, fp, N
   r_rev=r[::-1]
   fr=np.fft.rfft(r_rev,N)
   c=np.fft.irfft(fp*fr,N)[:conv_len]
   A=c[(m-1)-np.arange(n)]
   return q, A, fp, N
```

Now, implementing `_improve_profile` robustly. Let's write pseudo fully:
```
def _improve_profile(p0, max_time, rng, c_soft=20.0, alpha=1.0, tag='', floor=1e-12):
    p = np.asarray(p0, dtype=float)
    n = p.size
    p = np.maximum(p,0.0)
    s=p.sum()
    if s<=0 or not finite: return p0/len(p0), inf
    p=p/s
    best=p.copy(); best_score=_internal_score(p)
    alpha_cur=alpha
    stale=0
    # precompute N
    conv_len=3*n-2; N=1; while N<conv_len: N*=2
    t_start=time.monotonic()
    it=0
    while time.monotonic() - t_start < max_time:
        q, fp, _ = _fft_square_and_forward(p, N)
        qmax=float(np.max(q))
        score=2.0*n*qmax
        if not np.isfinite(score): break
        if score < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score=score; best=p.copy(); stale=0
        else:
            stale += 1
        if stale > 200:
            # reduce alpha and perturb
            alpha_cur = max(0.05, alpha_cur*0.5)
            # perturbrange from best
            base = np.clip(best, 1e-15, None)
            noise = rng.normal(0.0, 0.15, n)
            cand = base * np.exp(noise)
            p = _proj_simplex(cand)
            stale = 0
            continue  # next loop recompute
        # gradient
        theta = c_soft * (q / max(qmax, 1e-300) - 1.0)
        # theta max 0; subtract max? max is 0 (maybe due to qmax q has max, floating). Do theta -= np.max(theta)
        theta -= np.max(theta)
        # To avoid underflow if c_soft large? c_soft 20 so okay.
        r = np.exp(theta)
        rsum = r.sum()
        if rsum <= 0: r = np.ones(2*n-1)/(2*n-1)
        else: r = r/rsum
        A = _fft_cross_with_forward_r(r, fp, N, n)
        G = A + p  # rsum 1
        Gbar = float(np.dot(p, G))
        grad = G - Gbar
        sc = float(np.max(np.abs(grad)))
        if sc < 1e-15:
            # small gradient, maybe try random perturbation
            if it % 10 == 0:
                base=np.clip(p,1e-15,None); p=_proj_simplex(base*np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.1,n)))
            else:
                it +=1; continue
        step = (alpha_cur / n) * (grad / sc)
        p = _proj_simplex(p - step)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(p)):
            p = best.copy()
        # adjust alpha: if score improved (stale==0) increase, else slow decay
        if stale == 0:
            alpha_cur = min(8.0, alpha_cur * 1.02)
        else:
            alpha_cur = max(0.2, alpha_cur * 0.999)
        it += 1
        if it % 200 == 0:
            print(tag, "iter", it, "score", best_score, "file")
    p = best.copy()
    return p, best_score
```
But `stale` increments when current not improve; after a non-improving step, stale>0, then we don't perturb until 200. Adaptive alpha decays slowly. Good.
`theta = c_soft*(q/qmax -1)`. Since qmax max, numerator max 0, min negative. `-1`? q/qmax -1 ranges [-1,0]. If qmax positive, theta = c_soft*(negative) okay. But if q has values slightly >qmax due to max? no. Do `theta = -c_soft * (1.0 - q/qmax)`; max 0.
`q` may have small negative from FFT; `np.maximum(q,0)`. Good.
If qmax 0 (not), return.

Alpha adjustment: If score improves, best_score updated and stale=0; increasing alpha okay. If no improve, decays. However after we do continue on stale>200, alpha halved. Fine.

Maybe okay. But if alpha too large relative to p, gradient direction normalized could push many to zero. To prevent, set max alpha maybe 2.0? Let's think. Direction entries in [-1,1], step=alpha/n.
```
def _fft_square_and_forward(a, N=None):
    n=len(a); m=2*n-1; need=max(m,3*n-2)
    if N is None or N < need: N=_next_pow2(need)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    q=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, N)[:m]
    q=np.asarray(np.real(q), dtype=np.float64)
    np.maximum(q, 0.0, out=q)
    return q, fa, N
```
But `fa` length N//2+1. For cross, need length 3n-2; if N computed need max. Good. For score-only `_internal_score(a)`:
```
def _internal_score(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float); n=len(a); s=a.sum()
    if n==0 or s<=0 or not finite: return inf
    p=a/s
    q,_,_ = _fft_square_and_forward(p)
    return 2.0*n*float(np.max(q))
```

```
if n<128: q=np.convolve(p,p)
else FFT
```

```
def _fft_cross_with_forward_r(r, fp, N, n):
    m=2*n-1
    if N < 3*n-2: N=_next_pow2(3*n-2); fp = np.fft.rfft(np.zeros? ) # can't recompute? assume N enough.
    r_rev = r[::-1]
    fr=np.fft.rfft(r_rev, N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fp*fr, N)[:3*n-2]
    A=c[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
    return A
```

Let's test the correlation formula thoroughly.
Derivative G_i = ∑_k r_k p_{k-i} + p_i ∑ r_k.
A_i = ∑_k r_k p_{k-i}. For k-i between 0,n-1. Let r length m=2n-1. We compute p * r_rev. r_rev[t]=r[m-1-t]. Convolution c[s]=∑_{j=0}^{n-1} p[j] r_rev[s-j]. We need s = m-1 - i? Let's re-evaluate with indices: r_rev[s-j]=r[m-1-(s-j)] = r[m-1-s+j]. We want r[i+j]. Need m-1-s+j = i+j => s=m-1-i. Yes. So A_i=c[m-1-i]. c array length n+m-1 = n+2n-1-1=3n-2, indices 0..3n-3? length L=3n-2 max index 3n-3. m-1-i max: m-1=2n-2, i=0 =>2n-2; min i=n-1 => n-1. These lie in [n-1, 2n-2]. Our slice c[n-1:2n-1] gives indices n-1..2n-2. Reverse gives [2n-2..n-1]. Good.
Check sum of A? If r uniform sum1, A_i = (1/m)∑_k p_{k-i} ≈ p? Some. Not important.

Could there be an off-by-one because convolution sum over i in np.convolve includes all valid j? Yes.
For n=1, m=1, c length1? p length1, r_rev length1, c[0]=p r0. Slice c[0:1][::-1] ok. G=A+p=r0p+p=2p if r0=1. q=[p^2]. gradient of logsumexp q wrt p? q=p^2, dq=2p, yes.

Now, `_proj_simplex` projection: The direction step includes constants; after projection, sum exactly 1. Need if v all -inf? use finite. Implementation:
```
def _proj_simplex(v):
    v=np.asarray(v,float).copy()
    if v.size==0: return v
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(v)): return np.ones(v.size)/v.size
    u=np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv=np.cumsum(u)
    rho=np.arange(1, v.size+1, dtype=np.float64)
    cond= u*rho - cssv > 0
    if not np.any(cond):
       w=np.maximum(v,0.0); s=w.sum(); ...
    k=int(np.nonzero(cond)[0][-1])
    theta=cssv[k]/(k+1.0)
    w=np.maximum(v-theta,0.0)
    s=w.sum()
    if s<=0: return ones/n
    return w/s
```
Even if cond none, return normalized positive part. Good.

When up/f downsample, there is endpoint handling. Fine.

Let's refine `_run_step_search` with time checks.

```
def _run_step_search(L, deadline):
    top=[]
    cvals = np.arange(0.03, 0.98, 0.025)  # ~38
    rvals = np.unique(np.concatenate(([1.0], np.logspace(0, math.log10(30.0), 25))))
    evals=0
    for c in cvals:
       if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
       for r in rvals:
          p=_make_step_profile(L,float(c),float(r))
          sc=_internal_score(p)
          _add_top(top,sc,float(c),float(r),5)
          evals +=1
          if evals > 2000: break
       if evals>2000: break
    # refine top 3 with SciPy if available
    if _HAVE_SO:
       for sc,c,r in top[:3]:
           if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
           try:
               res=so.minimize(_step_objective, [c,r], args=(L,), method='Nelder-Mead', options={'maxiter':200,'xatol':1e-4,'fatol':1e-6})
               if res.x is not None:
                   rr=sc._step_objective(res.x,L)
                   _add_top(top, rr, float(res.x[0]), float(res.x[1]),5)
           except Exception:
               pass
    top.sort()
    profiles=[]
    for sc,c,r in top[:5]:
       profiles.append(_make_step_profile(L,c,r))
    return profiles
```
But in print use `print('step search best', top[0][0])`.

```
             res=so.minimize(_harmonic_objective, [a0,b0], args=(L,), method='Nelder-Mead', options={'maxiter':100,'xatol':1e-3,'fatol':1e-5})
             x=res.x; sc=_harmonic_objective(x,L); add profile if finite
          except: pass
    else:
       grid a,b -1..1 step .25 with a^2+b^2<=1.5? add top?
```

Need top-level `_valid_profile(p)`:
```
def _valid_profile(p):
   a=np.asarray(p,float).flatten()
   if a.size==0 or not finite: return None
   a=np.maximum(a,0)
   s=a.sum()
   if s<=0: return None
   return a/s
```

```
try: a=np.asarray(seq,float); a=np.maximum(a,0); s=a.sum(); if finite and s>0 and size>0: return _resample_profile(a/s,L) else None
```

```
def _run_step_search(L_coarse, L_refine, deadline):
    top=[]
    cvals = np.arange(0.03,0.98,0.025)
    rvals = np.unique(np.concatenate(([1.0], np.logspace(0, math.log10(30.0), 25))))
    for ... score at L_coarse add
    # refine top 3 at L_refine
    for sc,c,r in top[:3]:
       c_lo=max(0.01,c-0.06); c_hi=min(0.99,c+0.06)
       c_range=np.arange(c_lo,c_hi+1e-9,0.004)
       r_lo=max(1.0,r-0.3); r_hi=min(50.0,r+0.3) # if r large need relative? use r*exp
       r_range=np.arange(r_lo,r_hi+1e-9,0.025) if r<5 else np.arange(r_lo,r_hi+1e-9,r*0.02)
       for c1 in c_range:
          for r1 in r_range:
             score at L_refine add
    # If refine adds new top, refine top again? Could do second round top[:2] at finer step 0.001/0.01 if time.
    if time < deadline:
       for sc,c,r in top[:2]:
          c_range=np.arange(c-0.02,c+0.02+1e-9,0.001)
          r_range relative step 0.005
       ...
    # optional scipy
    return profiles length L_refine (top[:5])
```

Let's sketch `_prepare_start_pool`:
```
def _prepare_start_pool(L_coarse, L_mid, deadline, rng):
    starts=[]
    # step search
    step_deadline = deadline
    step_profiles = _run_step_search(L_coarse, L_mid, step_deadline)
    starts.extend(step_profiles)
    # harmonic if time
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 10.0:
       harm_profiles = _run_harmonic_search(L_mid, min(deadline, time.monotonic()+20.0))
       starts.extend(harm_profiles)
    # random
    for _ in range(2):
       starts.append(_make_random_profile(L_mid, rng))
    # height
    hs = globals().get('height_sequence_1')
    p = _valid_downsample(hs, L_mid)
    if p is not None: starts.append(p)
    # score/unique? sort
    scored=[]
    for p in starts:
       pv=_valid_profile(p)
       if pv is None: continue
       sc=_internal_score(pv)
       scored.append((sc,pv))
```

Random profile function:
```
def _make_random_profile(L, rng):
    x=(np.arange(L)+0.5)/L
    f=1.0 + rng.uniform(-0.5,0.5)*(x-0.5)
    for k in range(1,5):
       f += rng.uniform(0.0,0.3)*np.sin(2*np.pi*k*x + rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi))
    f=np.maximum(f,1e-8)
    return f/f.sum()
```

Let's detail `_run_step_search` with no lambdas:
```
def _run_step_search(L_coarse, L_refine, deadline):
    top=[]
    cvals = np.arange(0.03, 0.98, 0.025)
    rvals = np.unique(np.concatenate(([1.0], np.logspace(0.0, math.log10(30.0), 25))))
    for c in cvals:
       if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
       for r in rvals:
          sc = _step_score(int(L_coarse), float(c), float(r))
          _add_top(top, sc, float(c), float(r), 6)
    # refine
    for _round in range(2):
       if not top: break
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 5: break
       for sc,c,r in top[:3 if _round==0 else 2]:
          c_lo=max(0.01,c-(0.06 if _round==0 else 0.02))
          c_hi=min(0.99,c+(0.06 if _round==0 else 0.02))
          c_step=0.004 if _round==0 else 0.001
          c_range=np.arange(c_lo,c_hi+1e-9,c_step)
          r_lo=max(1.0, r/(1.6 if _round==0 else 1.2))
          r_hi=min(50.0, r*(1.6 if _round==0 else 1.2))
          if r_hi <= r_lo: r_range=np.array([r])
          else: r_range=np.logspace(math.log10(r_lo), math.log10(r_hi), 15 if _round==0 else 11)
          for c1 in c_range:
             if time>deadline: break
             for r1 in r_range:
                sc1 = _step_score(int(L_refine), float(c1), float(r1))
                _add_top(top, sc1, float(c1), float(r1), 6)
    # scipy
    if _HAVE_SO and time.monotonic() < deadline - 10:
       for sc,c,r in top[:3]:
          try:
             res = scipy.optimize.minimize(_step_objective, [c,r], args=(L_refine,), method='Nelder-Mead', options={'maxiter':120,'xatol':1e-4,'fatol':1e-6})
             sc2 = _step_objective(res.x, L_refine)
             _add_top(top, sc2, float(res.x[0]), float(res.x[1]), 6)
          except Exception: pass
    top.sort()
    profiles=[]
    for sc,c,r in top[:4]:
       profiles.append(_make_step_profile(int(L_refine), c, r))
    if profiles and _HAVE_SO: print('step best', top[0][0])
    return profiles
```
Need import `scipy.optimize` as `so` to avoid attribute issues. Use `so.minimize` if `_HAVE_SO`.

```

def _fft_cross_with_forward_r(r, fp, N, n):
    m = 2*n - 1
    conv_len = 3*n - 2
    if N < conv_len:
        # cannot recompute fp here because p not available; caller should pass N large
        N = _next_pow2(conv_len)
        # but fp length mismatch; to be safe, fallback direct?
```

```
fr = np.fft.rfft(r_rev, N)
c = np.fft.irfft(fp * fr, N)[:conv_len]
return c[n-1:2*n-1][::-1].copy()
```

Now, `_improve_profile` code in detail:
```
def _improve_profile(p0, max_time, rng, c_soft=20.0, alpha=0.6, tag=''):
    start=time.monotonic()
    p = _valid_profile(p0)
    if p is None:
        n = int(np.size(p0)) if p0 is not None else 256
        p = np.ones(max(1,n), dtype=np.float64)/max(1,n)
    n=p.size
    if max_time <=0 or n ==0:
        return p, _internal_score(p)
    need=3*n-2
    N=1; while N<need: N <<=1
    best=p.copy(); best_score=inf
    alpha_cur=float(alpha)
    stale=0
    it=0
    last_print=start
    t_limit=start+float(max_time)
    while time.monotonic() < t_limit:
        q, fp, _ = _fft_square_and_forward(p, N)
        if q.size ==0: break
        qmax=float(np.max(q))
        if not np.isfinite(qmax) or qmax <=0: break
        score=2.0*float(n)*qmax
        if score < best_score:
            best_score=score; best=p.copy(); stale=0
        else:
            stale +=1
        if stale > 300:
            base=np.maximum(best, 1e-15)
            noise=rng.normal(0.0, 0.25, n)
            cand=base*np.exp(noise)
            p=_proj_simplex(cand)
            alpha_cur=max(0.1, alpha_cur*0.25)
            stale=0
            it +=1
            continue
        ratio=q/qmax
        x=-c_soft*(1.0-ratio)
        x=np.minimum(x,0.0)
        # Normalize r
        x_max=float(np.max(x))
        if not np.isfinite(x_max): x_max=0.0
        x = x - x_max
        r=np.exp(x)
        rs=float(np.sum(r))
        if rs <=0 or not np.isfinite(rs):
            r=np.ones(q.size)/q.size
        else:
            r=r/rs
        A=_fft_cross_with_forward_r(r, fp, N, n)
        G=A + p
        Gbar=float(np.dot(p,G))
        grad=G-Gbar
        sc=float(np.max(np.abs(grad)))
        if not np.isfinite(sc) or sc <= 1e-15:
            # noise to escape
            base=np.maximum(p,1e-15)
            p=_proj_simplex(base*np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,0.1,n)))
            it +=1
            continue
        step=(alpha_cur/float(n))*(grad/sc)
        p_new=_proj_simplex(p - step)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(p_new)):
            p_new=best.copy()
        p=p_new
        if stale == 0:
            alpha_cur = min(2.0, alpha_cur*1.05)
        else:
            alpha_cur = max(0.1, alpha_cur*0.995)
        it +=1
        if it % 100 == 0:
            now=time.monotonic()
            if now-last_print > 3.0:
                print(tag, 'iter', it, 'best', best_score, 'file')
                last_print=now
    return best, best_score
```
Wait `stale` after first iteration initially inf; score<inf => stale=0 and alpha increase. After a non-improving step, stale=1 and alpha decays. Good. But if first iteration best inf, then continue loop; after update p, next iteration compute. Fine.
Potential issue: `best_score` after internal FFT may be slightly different than exact; okay.
If `max_time` small, maybe only one iteration.

If `stale>300`, we perturb from best but don't update best_score? Next loop. Fine.

Could the best_score remain inf if first q invalid? break and return p,inf. In propose, if score inf, fallback. Add initial best_score from `_internal_score(p)` before loop to avoid.
```
best_score = _internal_score(p)
best=p.copy()
```
This uses extra conv but okay. Then in loop if score < best_score update. Good. Use this to ensure finite.

```python
"""
Search for low-peak nonnegative step-function coefficients.

Strategy:
- The objective is 2*n*max(conv(a,a))/(sum(a)^2), scale invariant.
- Strong initial constructions come from one-step/asymmetric density profiles,
  which often already give scores well below 2.0.
- These profiles are refined by a projected/mirror-descent style local search
  using FFT for the convolution and a smooth max-constraint surrogate.
- Final candidates are upsampled to several lengths and validated with the
  official evaluate_sequence when available.
"""

import time
import math
import numpy as np

try:
    import scipy.optimize as so
    _HAVE_SO = True
except Exception:
    _HAVE_SO = False


def _next_pow2(m):
    n = 1
    while n < m:
        n <<= 1
    return n


def _fft_square_and_forward(a, N=None):
    """Return linear square convolution and the forward FFT of a."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    need = 3 * n - 2
    if N is None or N < need:
        N = _next_pow2(need)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    q = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)[: (2 * n - 1)]
    q = np.asarray(q, dtype=np.float64)
    np.maximum(q, 0.0, out=q)
    return q, fa, N


def _fft_cross_with_forward_r(r, fp, N, n):
    """
    Given r_k = weights on convolution indices 0..2n-2 and fp=FFT(p),
    compute A_i = sum_k r_k * p_{k-i}.
    """
    conv_len = 3 * n - 2
    r_rev = r[::-1]
    fr = np.fft.rfft(r_rev, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fp * fr, N)[:conv_len]
    # desired indices are n-1 .. 2n-2 in reverse
    A = c[n - 1:2 * n - 1][::-1]
    return A


def _proj_simplex(v):
    """Projection onto the probability simplex."""
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    n = v.size
    if n == 0:
        return v
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(v)):
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)

    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv = np.cumsum(u)
    rho = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    cond = (u * rho) - cssv > 0.0

    if not np.any(cond):
        w = np.maximum(v, 0.0)
        s = float(w.sum())
        if s > 0.0 and np.isfinite(s):
            return w / s
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)

    idx = np.nonzero(cond)[0]
    k = int(idx[-1])
    theta = cssv[k] / float(k + 1)
    w = np.maximum(v - theta, 0.0)
    s = float(w.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    return w / s


def _internal_score(a):
    """Fast internal proxy for evaluate_sequence, using FFT for larger n."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        return float("inf")
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return float("inf")
    p = a / s

    if n <= 512:
        q = np.convolve(p, p)
        qmax = float(np.max(q)) if q.size else 0.0
    else:
        q, _, _ = _fft_square_and_forward(p)
        qmax = float(np.max(q)) if q.size else 0.0

    if not np.isfinite(qmax):
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * n * qmax)


def _valid_profile(p):
    """Return a normalized, finite, nonnegative profile or None."""
    if p is None:
        return None
    a = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return None
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        return None
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return None
    return a / s


def _resample_profile(p_from, n_to):
    """Resample a normalized profile to another length, preserving density."""
    p_from = _valid_profile(p_from)
    if p_from is None:
        return np.ones(int(n_to), dtype=np.float64) / float(n_to)

    n_from = p_from.size
    n_to = max(1, int(n_to))
    if n_from == n_to:
        return p_from.copy()
    if n_from == 1:
        return np.full(n_to, float(p_from[0]), dtype=np.float64)

    x_old = (np.arange(n_from) + 0.5) / float(n_from)
    x_new = (np.arange(n_to) + 0.5) / float(n_to)

    # Work in density units: f = n * p.
    f_old = p_from * float(n_from)
    f_new = np.interp(x_new, x_old, f_old)
    f_new = np.maximum(f_new, 0.0)
    s = float(f_new.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.ones(n_to, dtype=np.float64) / float(n_to)
    return f_new / s


def _make_step_profile(L, c, r):
    """
    One-step asymmetric profile:
        f(x) = r if x < c, else 1
    normalized in coefficient space.
    """
    L = int(max(1, L))
    try:
        c = float(c)
        r = float(r)
    except Exception:
        c = 0.25
        r = 1.5
    c = min(max(c, 1e-4), 1.0 - 1e-4)
    r = min(max(r, 1e-3), 1e4)

    x = (np.arange(L) + 0.5) / float(L)
    f = np.where(x < c, r, 1.0)
    f = np.maximum(f, 1e-12)
    s = float(f.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64) / float(L)
    return f / s


def _make_harmonic_profile(L, a, b):
    """Profile f(x) = 1 + a cos(2*pi*x) + b sin(2*pi*x), clipped and normalized."""
    L = int(max(1, L))
    x = (np.arange(L) + 0.5) / float(L)
    f = 1.0 + float(a) * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * x) + float(b) * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * x)
    f = np.maximum(f, 1e-9)
    s = float(f.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64) / float(L)
    return f / s


def _make_random_profile(L, rng):
    """Smooth random nonnegative profile."""
    L = int(max(1, L))
    x = (np.arange(L) + 0.5) / float(L)
    f = 1.0 + rng.uniform(-0.45, 0.45) * (x - 0.5)
    for k in range(1, 5):
        amp = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 0.28))
        ph = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        f += amp * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * k * x + ph)
    f = np.maximum(f, 1e-9)
    s = float(f.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64) / float(L)
    return f / s


def _step_objective(x, L):
    try:
        c = float(x[0])
        r = float(x[1])
        p = _make_step_profile(L, c, r)
        sc = _internal_score(p)
        return sc if np.isfinite(sc) else 1e100
    except Exception:
        return 1e100


def _harmonic_objective(x, L):
    try:
        a = float(x[0])
        b = float(x[1])
        p = _make_harmonic_profile(L, a, b)
        sc = _internal_score(p)
        return sc if np.isfinite(sc) else 1e100
    except Exception:
        return 1e100


def _add_top(top, score, c, r, k=6):
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        return
    item = (float(score), float(c), float(r))
    if len(top) < k:
        top.append(item)
        top.sort()
    else:
        if item[0] < top[-1][0]:
            top[-1] = item
            top.sort()


def _run_step_search(L_coarse, L_refine, deadline):
    """Two-stage grid/optimizer search over one-step profiles."""
    top = []
    cvals = np.arange(0.03, 0.98, 0.025)
    rvals = np.unique(np.concatenate(([1.0], np.logspace(0.0, math.log10(30.0), 25))))

    # Coarse scan.
    for c in cvals:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break
        for r in rvals:
            p = _make_step_profile(L_coarse, c, r)
            sc = _internal_score(p)
            _add_top(top, sc, float(c), float(r), 6)

    # Local refinement at higher resolution.
    for rnd in range(2):
        if not top:
            break
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break
        take = 3 if rnd == 0 else 2
        for sc, c, r in top[:take]:
            c_lo = max(0.01, c - (0.06 if rnd == 0 else 0.02))
            c_hi = min(0.99, c + (0.06 if rnd == 0 else 0.02))
            c_step = 0.004 if rnd == 0 else 0.001
            if c_hi <= c_lo:
                c_range = np.array([c], dtype=float)
            else:
                c_range = np.arange(c_lo, c_hi + 1e-9, c_step)

            fac_lo = 1.6 if rnd == 0 else 1.2
            fac_hi = 1.6 if rnd == 0 else 1.2
            r_lo = max(1.0, r / fac_lo)
            r_hi = min(50.0, r * fac_hi)
            nr = 15 if rnd == 0 else 11
            if r_hi <= r_lo:
                r_range = np.array([r], dtype=float)
            else:
                r_range = np.logspace(math.log10(r_lo), math.log10(r_hi), nr)

            for c1 in c_range:
                if time.monotonic() > deadline:
                    break
                for r1 in r_range:
                    p = _make_step_profile(L_refine, c1, r1)
                    sc1 = _internal_score(p)
                    _add_top(top, sc1, float(c1), float(r1), 6)

    # Optional SciPy polish for the best few.
    if _HAVE_SO and time.monotonic() < deadline - 8.0:
        for sc, c, r in top[:3]:
            try:
                res = so.minimize(
                    _step_objective,
                    np.array([c, r], dtype=float),
                    args=(L_refine,),
                    method="Nelder-Mead",
                    options={"maxiter": 120, "xatol": 1e-5, "fatol": 1e-6},
                )
                if res is not None and res.x is not None:
                    sc2 = _step_objective(res.x, L_refine)
                    _add_top(top, sc2, float(res.x[0]), float(res.x[1]), 6)
            except Exception:
                pass

    top.sort()
    profiles = []
    for sc, c, r in top[:4]:
        profiles.append(_make_step_profile(L_refine, c, r))
    if not profiles:
        profiles.append(_make_step_profile(L_refine, 0.25, 1.5))
    if top:
        print("step_search_best_score", top[0][0])
    return profiles


def _run_harmonic_search(L, deadline):
    """Small auxiliary search over a one-harmonic profile for diversity."""
    top = []
    for a in np.arange(-1.0, 1.01, 0.25):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break
        for b in np.arange(-1.0, 1.01, 0.25):
            if math.hypot(float(a), float(b)) > 1.05:
                continue
            p = _make_harmonic_profile(L, a, b)
            sc = _internal_score(p)
            _add_top(top, sc, float(a), float(b), 4)

    if _HAVE_SO and time.monotonic() < deadline - 5.0:
        for sc, a, b in top[:2]:
            try:
                res = so.minimize(
                    _harmonic_objective,
                    np.array([a, b], dtype=float),
                    args=(L,),
                    method="Nelder-Mead",
                    options={"maxiter": 80, "xatol": 1e-4, "fatol": 1e-6},
                )
                if res is not None and res.x is not None:
                    sc2 = _harmonic_objective(res.x, L)
                    a2 = float(res.x[0])
                    b2 = float(res.x[1])
                    _add_top(top, sc2, a2, b2, 4)
            except Exception:
                pass

    top.sort()
    profiles = []
    for sc, a, b in top[:3]:
        profiles.append(_make_harmonic_profile(L, a, b))
    return profiles


def _valid_downsample_from_global(L):
    try:
        seq = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
        if seq is None:
            return None
        a = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if a.size == 0:
            return None
        a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
        s = float(a.sum())
        if s <= 1e-15 or not np.isfinite(s):
            return None
        return _resample_profile(a / s, L)
    except Exception:
        return None


def _prepare_start_pool(L_coarse, L_mid, deadline, rng):
    starts = []

    step_profiles = _run_step_search(L_coarse, L_mid, deadline)
    starts.extend(step_profiles)

    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 10.0:
        harm_profiles = _run_harmonic_search(L_mid, min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 20.0))
        starts.extend(harm_profiles)

    # Diversity starts.
    for _ in range(2):
        if time.monotonic() < deadline:
            starts.append(_make_random_profile(L_mid, rng))

    hs = _valid_downsample_from_global(L_mid)
    if hs is not None:
        starts.append(hs)

    scored = []
    for p in starts:
        pv = _valid_profile(p)
        if pv is None:
            continue
        sc = _internal_score(pv)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            scored.append((sc, pv))
    if not scored:
        scored = [(2.0, _make_step_profile(L_mid, 0.25, 1.5))]

    scores_arr = np.array([x[0] for x in scored], dtype=np.float64)
    order = np.argsort(scores_arr)
    out = [scored[int(i)][1] for i in order[:6]]
    if not out:
        out = [_make_step_profile(L_mid, 0.25, 1.5)]
    return out


def _improve_profile(p0, max_time, rng, c_soft=20.0, alpha=0.6, tag=""):
    """
    Local search on the simplex.

    Uses a smooth log-sum-exp surrogate of max convolution and a projected step.
    """
    p = _valid_profile(p0)
    if p is None:
        n = int(np.size(p0)) if p0 is not None else 512
        p = np.ones(max(1, n), dtype=np.float64) / float(max(1, n))

    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return p, float("inf")

    initial_score = _internal_score(p)
    if max_time <= 0.0:
        return p, initial_score

    t_start = time.monotonic()
    t_limit = t_start + float(max_time)
    need = 3 * n - 2
    N = _next_pow2(need)

    best = p.copy()
    best_score = initial_score if np.isfinite(initial_score) else 1e100
    alpha_cur = float(alpha)
    stale = 0
    it = 0
    last_print = t_start

    while time.monotonic() < t_limit:
        q, fp, _ = _fft_square_and_forward(p, N)
        if q.size == 0:
            break
        qmax = float(np.max(q))
        if not np.isfinite(qmax) or qmax <= 0.0:
            break
        score = float(2.0 * n * qmax)

        if np.isfinite(score) and score < best_score:
            best_score = score
            best = p.copy()
            stale = 0
        else:
            stale += 1

        # Periodic escape from stale basins.
        if stale > 300:
            base = np.maximum(best, 1e-15)
            noise = rng.normal(0.0, 0.25, n)
            cand = base * np.exp(noise)
            p = _proj_simplex(cand)
            alpha_cur = max(0.1, alpha_cur * 0.25)
            stale = 0
            it += 1
            continue

        # Smooth peak weights.
        ratio = q / qmax
        x = -c_soft * (1.0 - ratio)
        x = np.minimum(x, 0.0)
        xmax = float(np.max(x))
        if not np.isfinite(xmax):
            xmax = 0.0
        x = x - xmax
        r = np.exp(x)
        rs = float(np.sum(r))
        if rs <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(rs):
            r = np.ones(q.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(q.size)
        else:
            r = r / rs

        A = _fft_cross_with_forward_r(r, fp, N, n)
        G = A + p
        Gbar = float(np.dot(p, G))
        grad = G - Gbar
        sc = float(np.max(np.abs(grad)))

        if not np.isfinite(sc) or sc <= 1e-15:
            base = np.maximum(p, 1e-15)
            p = _proj_simplex(base * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.1, n)))
            it += 1
            continue

        step = (alpha_cur / float(n)) * (grad / sc)
        p_new = _proj_simplex(p - step)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(p_new)):
            p_new = best.copy()
        p = p_new

        if stale == 0:
            alpha_cur = min(2.0, alpha_cur * 1.05)
        else:
            alpha_cur = max(0.1, alpha_cur * 0.995)

        it += 1
        now = time.monotonic()
        if it % 100 == 0 and now - last_print > 3.0:
            print(tag, "iter", it, "best_internal", float(best_score))
            last_print = now

    return best, float(best_score)


def _add_candidate(cands, p, max_len=10000):
    pv = _valid_profile(p)
    if pv is None:
        return
    if pv.size < 16 or pv.size > max_len:
        return
    cands.append(pv)


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    """Main entry point: run search and return the best sequence found."""
    t_start = time.monotonic()
    budget = max(30.0, float(budget_s))
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    if budget >= 600.0:
        L_coarse = 1024
        L_mid = 2048
        N_final = 6733
        search_sec = min(120.0, max(45.0, 0.08 * budget))
        final_sec = max(300.0, 0.38 * budget)
        exact_sec = max(25.0, 0.02 * budget)
    elif budget >= 150.0:
        L_coarse = 512
        L_mid = 1024
        N_final = 4096
        search_sec = min(45.0, 0.15 * budget)
        final_sec = max(60.0, 0.35 * budget)
        exact_sec = max(12.0, 0.08 * budget)
    else:
        L_coarse = 256
        L_mid = 512
        N_final = 2048
        search_sec = 15.0
        final_sec = max(20.0, 0.30 * budget)
        exact_sec = 8.0

    mid_sec = max(30.0, budget - search_sec - final_sec - exact_sec - 5.0)

    t_end = t_start + budget - 1.0
    search_deadline = t_start + search_sec
    final_deadline = t_start + budget - exact_sec - 5.0
    mid_deadline = min(t_start + search_sec + mid_sec, final_deadline - 10.0)
    if mid_deadline < t_start + 5.0:
        mid_deadline = t_start + 5.0

    print("budget", budget, "search_sec", search_sec, "mid_sec", mid_sec,
          "final_sec", final_sec, "exact_sec", exact_sec)

    start_profiles = _prepare_start_pool(L_coarse, L_mid, search_deadline, rng)

    profiles = []
    scores = []
    for p in start_profiles:
        p = _valid_profile(_resample_profile(p, L_mid))
        if p is None:
            continue
        sc = _internal_score(p)
        if not np.isfinite(sc):
            continue
        profiles.append(p)
        scores.append(sc)

    if not profiles:
        p0 = _make_step_profile(L_mid, 0.25, 1.5)
        profiles = [p0]
        scores = [_internal_score(p0)]
        if not np.isfinite(scores[0]):
            scores = [2.0]

    order = np.argsort(np.array(scores, dtype=np.float64))
    profiles = [profiles[int(i)] for i in order[:4]]
    scores = [scores[int(i)] for i in order[:4]]
    best_global = float(min(scores))
    print("start_best_internal", best_global)

    # Mid-resolution round-robin improvement.
    if time.monotonic() < mid_deadline:
        while time.monotonic() < mid_deadline:
            order = np.argsort(np.array(scores, dtype=np.float64))
            profiles = [profiles[int(i)] for i in order]
            scores = [scores[int(i)] for i in order]

            if len(profiles) > 3:
                top3 = order[:3]
                profiles = [profiles[int(i)] for i in top3]
                scores = [scores[int(i)] for i in top3]

            for i in range(len(profiles)):
                if time.monotonic() >= mid_deadline:
                    break
                remaining = max(0.0, mid_deadline - time.monotonic())
                chunk = max(5.0, min(45.0, remaining / max(1, len(profiles))))
                original = profiles[i]
                p_new, sc = _improve_profile(
                    original,
                    max_time=chunk,
                    rng=rng,
                    c_soft=20.0,
                    alpha=0.6,
                    tag="mid",
                )
                if not np.isfinite(sc):
                    p_new = original
                    sc = _internal_score(p_new)
                if not np.isfinite(sc):
                    sc = best_global
                profiles[i] = p_new
                scores[i] = sc
                if sc < best_global:
                    best_global = sc
                    print("mid_improved_best", best_global)

    order = np.argsort(np.array(scores, dtype=np.float64))
    best_mid = profiles[int(order[0])]
    if np.isfinite(scores[int(order[0])]) and scores[int(order[0])] < best_global:
        best_global = scores[int(order[0])]

    second_mid = profiles[int(order[1])] if len(profiles) > 1 else None
    print("mid_best_internal", float(scores[int(order[0])]))

    final_candidates = []

    # Unpolished resampled candidates at a few lengths.
    ns = []
    if L_mid >= 512:
        ns.append(L_mid)
    if budget >= 300.0:
        ns.append(4096)
    ns.append(N_final)
    if budget >= 900.0:
        ns.append(8192)

    seen = set()
    for N in ns:
        if N in seen:
            continue
        seen.add(N)
        if N < 64 or N > 10000:
            continue
        _add_candidate(final_candidates, _resample_profile(best_mid, N), max_len=10000)

    if second_mid is not None and budget >= 600.0:
        _add_candidate(final_candidates, _resample_profile(second_mid, 4096), max_len=10000)
        _add_candidate(final_candidates, _resample_profile(second_mid, N_final), max_len=10000)

    # Final high-resolution polish on the best mid profile.
    p_pol = _valid_profile(_resample_profile(best_mid, N_final))
    if p_pol is not None:
        remaining_final = max(0.0, final_deadline - time.monotonic())
        if remaining_final > 30.0:
            p_pol, sc_pol = _improve_profile(
                p_pol,
                max_time=remaining_final - 5.0,
                rng=rng,
                c_soft=30.0,
                alpha=0.8,
                tag="final",
            )
            if not np.isfinite(sc_pol):
                p_pol = _resample_profile(best_mid, N_final)
            _add_candidate(final_candidates, p_pol, max_len=10000)
        else:
            _add_candidate(final_candidates, p_pol, max_len=10000)

    # Fallbacks.
    _add_candidate(final_candidates, best_mid, max_len=10000)
    _add_candidate(final_candidates, _make_step_profile(N_final, 0.25, 1.5), max_len=10000)
    _add_candidate(final_candidates, _make_step_profile(1024, 0.25, 1.5), max_len=10000)

    if not final_candidates:
        return [float(x) for x in _make_step_profile(1024, 0.25, 1.5)]

    # Rank by internal score first, then validate with exact evaluator.
    cand_internal = []
    for i, p in enumerate(final_candidates):
        sc = _internal_score(p)
        cand_internal.append((sc, i))
    scores_arr = np.array([x[0] for x in cand_internal], dtype=np.float64)
    order = np.argsort(scores_arr)

    exact_best = None
    exact_best_score = float("inf")
    eval_func = globals().get("evaluate_sequence", None)

    max_exact_calls = 8 if budget >= 900.0 else 5
    exact_time_budget = t_end - time.monotonic()

    for idx in order[:max_exact_calls]:
        if exact_time_budget <= 4.0:
            break
        ii = int(idx)
        p = final_candidates[ii]
        if p.size > 10000:
            continue
        seq = [float(x) for x in p]
        if eval_func is None:
            break
        try:
            sc_ex = float(eval_func(seq))
        except Exception:
            sc_ex = float("inf")
        if np.isfinite(sc_ex):
            if sc_ex < exact_best_score:
                exact_best_score = sc_ex
                exact_best = p.copy()
            print("exact_candidate_len", p.size, "exact_score", sc_ex)
        exact_time_budget = t_end - time.monotonic()

    if exact_best is not None:
        print("final_exact_best", exact_best_score)
        return [float(x) for x in exact_best]

    # If exact evaluation is unavailable/fails, return the best internal candidate.
    idx = int(order[0])
    best_internal = final_candidates[idx]
    return [float(x) for x in best_internal]
```
